""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

report_v2/qa/v3/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:73:report_v2/article/article.tex,report_v2/article/article.tex,64,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."

This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

report_v2/qa/v3/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:162:report_v2/article/v2/article.tex,report_v2/article/v2/article.tex,70,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

report_v2/qa/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:73:report_v2/article/article.tex,report_v2/article/article.tex,64,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."

Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."
